# Facts ledger

Status: **V** = verified by us (cite script/experiment). **S** = sourced from the community
(cite URL) — a claim to verify, not a fact we own. Add a row for every number we rely on.

| ID | Fact | Status | Source / verification |
|---|---|---|---|
| F1 | The TASVideos "warps" record is HappyLee's movie #1715M: **17,868 frames** = 4:57.31 (movie length at 60.0988 fps), published 2011-01-06, on `Super Mario Bros. (W) [!].nes` (fm2 says romFilename `Super Mario Bros. (JU) [!]`, emuVersion 9828, rerecordCount 9739). It obsoleted klmz's 4:57.33 by exactly 1 frame. | V (frame count) | https://tasvideos.org/1715M ; file `data/wr/happylee-supermariobros,warped.fm2`; `tools/fm2_info.py` |
| F2 | RTA-timing equivalent of #1715M is 4:54.032. | S | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Mario_Bros._speedrunning |
| F3 | Maru's "warps, RTA rules" (no Left+Right) TAS is 4:54.265 (4:57.54 TAS timing) — 14 frames slower than #1715M. | S | https://tasvideos.org/HomePages/Maru |
| F4 | Human any% WR: averge11 4:54.415 (2025-12-18). Humans match the TAS framerule in every level except 8-4 since Niftski's "Lightning 4-2" (Sept 2023). | S | https://gamerant.com/super-mario-bros-new-world-record-speedrun-january-2025/ ; Wikipedia |
| F5 | Level completion is quantized to a global 21-frame framerule; the time from flagpole to next level rounds up to the next boundary. 8-4 ends on the axe touch with no rounding. | S | https://tasvideos.org/GameResources/NES/SuperMarioBros |
| F6 | Fireworks fire when the timer's last digit is 1, 3, or 6 at the flagpole; they cost frames. This is why the TAS avoids the flagpole glitch in 8-3. | S | Game Resources page |
| F7 | TASVideos also publishes 12 other SMB1 branches, incl. warpless 18:36.78 (HappyLee & Mars608, 2018), warps (Europe/PAL) 4:55.16, walkathon variants, minimum presses, max score/coins, FDS "-3 stage ending" 2:44.61, FDS "game end glitch" 5:29.957, and "arbitrary code execution" 4:52.65. | S | https://tasvideos.org/1G |
| F8 | The ACE movie (OnehundredthCoin, 4:52.65) requires a cartridge hot-swap from SMB3 to seed RAM $07FD so SMB1 boots into "World N" (world index 22). Mechanism: world index ≥ 8 → Bowser-replacement table OOB read → object ID $C9 → behavior jump table → $D007 → state machine value 4 → jump to unmapped $53AE → open bus executes SRE ($0A),Y → RTI → PC lands at uninitialized $1181 → controller-driven payload. | S | https://tasvideos.org/8991S |
| F9 | Community belief: the NES Minus World (-1, world index 36) is a water level whose exit pipe loops back to itself; on FDS the minus worlds differ and allow a -3 ending and ACE. | S (belief, not proof) | TASVideos publications; re-examine under H6 |
| F10 | Pressing Left+Right for one frame makes Mario walk backward in the opposite direction, giving instant deceleration. Enemy hitboxes affect Mario only every other frame. 16 subpixels per pixel. | S | Game Resources page |
| F11 | A known 8-4 micro-improvement (RTA-rules TAS): in the turnaround room, stop on the floor one frame earlier while still scrolling far enough for the wrong warp; depends on subspeed (RAM $705). | S | https://www.speedrun.com/smb1/forums/1wd01 |
| F12 | zdoroviy_antony's 2019 "perfect subpixel optimization" .fm3 rewrite ends on exactly frame 17868 — no improvement. flamexx's 2025 rewrite from 4-2 and cpt32's 2025 warps TAS are both slower than #1715M. | S | https://tasvideos.org/Forum/Topics/1337?CurrentPage=59 ; https://tasvideos.org/userfiles/game/1 |
| F13 | The TASVideos SMB1 forum thread has 63 pages (1,558 posts); the last posts (Oct–Nov 2025) are about warpless 5-1 manipulation; no active warps-improvement effort is visible on pp.59–63. | S | https://tasvideos.org/Forum/Topics/1337?CurrentPage=63 |

| F16 | Structure of the WR input file: soft-reset command on frame 0; first Start press on frame 41 (0-based); last input on frame 17848 (0-based) = 17,849 (1-based), an A press; 19 input-free frames follow (the axe is reached during the coast). Left+Right is pressed on 85 frames; Up+Down on none. Hold counts: R 2718, L 405, D 7, U 0, Start 1, Select 0, B 1680, A 2596. | V | `tools/fm2_info.py data/wr/happylee-supermariobros,warped.fm2 --list-lr` |
| F17 | The published length (17,868) counts the 19 trailing input-free frames. So the bar for a new movie is: game completes AND last input earlier than frame 17848 (0-based); a shorter file alone is not an improvement. | V (from F16) + rule reading to confirm in P4 | TASVideos movie rules |

| F25 | Frame-index conventions: FCEUX Lua dump row i (after the i-th `emu.frameadvance()`) ↔ fm2 frame i−1 (verified by matching FCEUX's reported joypad byte to the fm2 on 17,800 rows, 0 mismatches); BizHawk row i ↔ fm2 frame i (one ahead; its `joypad.get` reads 0 during playback, alignment from lag rows). Boot RAM-init differs (FCEUX $FF, NesHawk $00) but the game overwrites it before it matters. The 1-2/4-2 pipe-intro areas last exactly 499 rows each. | V | `tools/check_sync.py data/wr/fceux_wr.csv …`; full per-frame RAM in `data/wr/fceux_wr.ram` (regenerable) |
| F27 | **Framerule mechanism (code + dump).** `DecTimers` (NMI, before game logic) decrements IntervalTimerControl ($077F) each frame; on underflow it resets to 20 and the interval timers $0796–$07A3 decrement → 21-frame period. A flag level ends when StarFlagTaskControl ($0746) reaches 5: task 3 sets the star-flag object's EnemyIntervalTimer = 6 on frame T_set, task 4 waits for it (and EventMusicBuffer = 0, never binding in the WR). With post-frame ITC = v at T_set the area change happens at T_set + (v+1) + 105 → slack = v, deficit = 21 − v. The time-bonus countdown costs **1 frame per timer unit**. Verified: predicted = observed expiry in all 5 flag levels. | V | `tools/slack_table.py`; `docs/experiments/P0.4-slack-table.md`; smbdis.asm `DecTimers`, `RunStarFlagObj`, `DelayToAreaEnd`, `PlayerEndLevel` |
| F28 | **Level-start law.** For main-level entries (AltEntranceControl = 0) the intermission card's ScreenTimer ($07A0) = 7 is written on frame load+8 (the load's lag frame is load+2); with post-frame ITC = w there, player control starts at **load + 154 + w** (= 8 + (w+1) + 6×21 + 19); exact for all six such loads in the WR. Flag levels load at u = 19 → w = 12 → 166 frames. Pipe levels' framerule is therefore set at the next load: slack = w, deficit = 21 − w: 1-2 (w=13 at the 4-1 load), 4-2 (w=8 at the 8-1 load). Sub-area loads (AltEntranceControl ≠ 0: 1-1 bonus room, 4-2 wrong warp, 8-4 rooms) have no card and are **not** quantized (control at load+43 / +122). | V | `tools/slack_table.py` area-load table |
| F29 | **WR slack table** (slack / deficit in frames): 1-1 20/**1**, 1-2 13/8, 4-1 12/9, 4-2 8/13, 8-1 3/18, 8-2 2/19, 8-3 11/10, 8-4 unquantized (axe). Per-level frames (load→next load): 1902, 1870, 2228, 1729, 3042, 2143, 2101, 2810 (+43 to first load). Flag glitch used in 1-1, 4-1, 8-1, 8-2; 8-3 slides. Timer at the end event: 370, 347, 340, 356, 200, 338, 244, 317. | V | `tools/slack_table.py` |
| F30 | All 24 lag frames of the WR are boot (7), the Start press (1) and one per area load (16); there is no in-level lag. Sub-area loads lag too. | V | `tools/check_sync.py`, `tools/slack_table.py` |
| F31 | **Boot/Start law.** The title menu (`GameMenuRoutine`, OperMode_Task 3) accepts Start from row 34 (fm2 frame 33); Start sets OperMode = 1 the same frame and that row is the 1-1 load row. With the level-start law (F28) every Start row 34–43 yields control on row 197; the WR presses on row 43. 1-1's control start is therefore fixed by the boot ITC phase (7 boot lag frames), not by the Start frame. | V | `docs/timing-model.md` §3; dump rows 1–46 |
| F32 | **Flag-level end sequence constants.** Flagpole glitch = grab with Mario Y ≥ $A2 (162): `FlagpoleRoutine` sets GES 5 the same frame (no slide). Glitch grab → castle-door trigger = 126 frames in 1-1/4-1/8-1 (8-2: 1 frame, the glitch happens at the door; 8-3 slides: 173 incl. walk). Task 1 completes instantly; countdown = T + 1 frames (T = timer at the grab); flag raise = 32 frames; then T_set. So **T_set = grab + T + 159** (glitch levels; 8-2: + T + 34) and the next load = T_set + (v+1) + 106. Fireworks only for timer last digit 1/3/6 (none in the WR). | V | `docs/timing-model.md` §6; dump (all 5 flag levels) |
| F33 | **Pipes and sub-areas.** Every down-pipe entry takes 48 frames (ChangeAreaTimer = $30) from GES 3 to the load; side pipes 160 (page 0) or 52. Sub-area entries: control at load+43 (side entry / intro→main), +122 (vertical pipe exit, 96-frame rise), +27 (4-2 wrong-warp re-entry). 1-2/4-2 intro areas: 499 rows, input overridden. | V | `docs/timing-model.md` §4/§7; dump |
| F34 | **Game timer.** Header loads 401; `RunGameTimer` ticks on the first control frame and then every 24 frames while GES ≥ 8 and Mario is on screen (pipes, loads, sub-area entrances pause it). T at the grab = 400 − floor(eligible control frames / 24). | V | `docs/timing-model.md` §5; dump 1-1 (31 ticks, deltas 24 except across the pipe/bonus room) |

No search record or proof exists in any source read. The 2019 subpixel rewrite "lost time in the third room of 1-1 to execute the flagpole glitch". | S | `docs/community-claims.md` (Topics/8414, speedrun.com 0jnl2) |

Humans' "Lightning 4-2" reaches the same framerule as the TAS with "zero frames to spare". | S | `docs/community-claims.md` (#2964S, speedrun.com elaz6/tk6gs/rla1w) |
| F37 | zdoroviy_antony: "8-3 with FPG and '242' is 3 frames faster than without FPG — 1 frame from walking to the castle and 2 frames from timer countdown" (the WR slides with timer 244). Maru's RTA-rules TAS stops "on the floor one frame earlier" in the 8-4 turnaround room (depends on subspeed $705). | S | `docs/community-claims.md` (speedrun.com 0jnl2, 1wd01) |
| F38 | **Area-load memory law.** `InitializeArea` → `InitializeMemory` (Y=$4B) clears $0000–$074B (not the stack $0160–$01FF) plus $0780–$07A1 on every area load incl. death restarts. Survivors: AreaPointer $0750, EntrancePage $0751, AltEntranceControl $0752, HalfwayPage $075B, LevelNumber $075C, WorldNumber $075F, AreaNumber $0760, ITC $077F, $07A2–$07FF. WarpZoneControl $06D6 and ScrollLock $0723 are cleared. | V | `docs/warp-model.md` §1; smbdis.asm `InitializeArea`, `InitializeMemory` |
| F39 | **Area pointer resolution.** `AreaPointer = AreaAddrOffsets[WorldAddrOffsets[WorldNumber] + AreaNumber]` ($9CB4/$9CBC, 8/36 bytes; worlds 1,2,4,7 have 5 areas because the pipe-intro scene $29 is an area). Pointer bits 6–5 = type, low 5 = index (ground $20+n = GroundArea n+1, etc.; bit 7 ignored). Recomputed only by `StartWorld1`, `NextArea` (flag/axe), `PlayerEndWorld`, `ContinueGame` (death / game-over continue). | V | `tools/warp_tables.py`; `docs/warp-model.md` §2–3 |
| F40 | **Pipe-destination law.** A down-pipe goes to `AreaPointer` at `EntrancePage` as last written by a row-$0E enemy command (3 bytes: pointer, world<<5 \| page) parsed when its column came within ScreenRight+48 px **and** its 3-bit world equalled WorldNumber — or to the warp lookup if WZC ≠ 0. All 58 commands are tabulated (`docs/warp-model.md` §3.3); only 8-4's own commands point at a castle and only the 8-4 water section's command writes the Bowser-room page 16. The WR's 4-2 wrong warp = the page-5-col-4 pipe entered before the page-5-col-15 command (→ $42) was parsed, so AreaPointer was still $2F from the page-2-col-1 (vine) command (dump load row 7223, mode 1). | V | `tools/area_data.py`; `tools/ram_trace.py`; `docs/warp-model.md` §3 |
| F41 | **Warp-zone law.** `ScrollLockObject_Warp` writes WZC = 4 (world 1) / 5 / 6 (worlds > 1, ground-type area) and toggles ScrollLock; enemy $34 `WarpZoneObject` does WZC += 1 once (when ScrollLock ≠ 0 and Player_Y & Player_Y_HighPos = 0) and erases itself; `HandlePipeEntry` reads `WarpZoneNumbers[(WZC&3)·4 + pipe]` (pipe 0/1/2 for X < $60 / < $A0 / else), WorldNumber = byte − 1, Area/Level/EntrancePage = 0, mode 0 (quantized, F28). **Reachable WZC at a pipe = {0,1,4,5,6}** (proof: `docs/warp-model.md` §5.4: text unreachable while locked, one self-erasing increment per visit, both cleared per load). Destinations: 1-2 {4,3,2} (WZC 4) or {−1,5,−1} (WZC 1); 4-2 ceiling zone 5; 4-2 $2F zone {8,7,6} (no $34 enemy there). World 8 only from 4-2. Dump: 1-2 WZC 0→1 (row 3546)→4 (3721); $2F 0→6 (7608), pipe row 7724 X 114 → world 8. | V | `tools/warp_tables.py`; `tools/ram_trace.py`; `docs/warp-model.md` §5 |
| F42 | **Bogus worlds.** WorldNumber 35 (NES Minus World, WZC 1 left/right pipe in 1-2) → `WorldAddrOffsets[35]` = $33 → `AreaAddrOffsets[51]` = $01 = Water2 (2-2/7-2 data); its commands need world 2/3/7 → never match → exit pipe reloads $01; death/continue recompute $01; no flagpole → closed loop at table level. WorldNumber 38/255 (WZC 3/7) are unreachable; they would load $00 / $9D (enemy data at $7EA0 = open bus). | V | `tools/warp_tables.py` §"bogus worlds"; `docs/warp-model.md` §6 |
| F43 | **Completion condition.** After the axe, `PlayerEndWorld` ends the game iff WorldNumber ≥ 7 (`cpy #World8; bcs`); otherwise Area/Level = 0, WorldNumber++, `LoadAreaPointer`. WorldNumber writers: `GoContinue` (title: 0, or `ContinueWorld` $07FD with A+Start — set only by a game over), `HandlePipeEntry`, `PlayerEndWorld`. So without memory corruption world 8 is first reachable at 4-2's $2F zone. | V | smbdis.asm `PlayerEndWorld`, `GoContinue`; `docs/warp-model.md` §0/§7 |
| F44 | **Boot.** Warm boot iff `TopScoreDisplay` digits ($07D7–$07DC) all < 10 and `WarmBootValidation` $07FF = $A5 (then memory is cleared only to $07D5). Power-on = cold boot: clears to $07FD, writes $A5. `ContinueWorld` and `WorldSelectEnableFlag` ($07FC) are 0 on power-on. | V | smbdis.asm `Start`; `docs/warp-model.md` §8 |
| F45 | **QuickNES is frame-exact on the WR.** libretro QuickNES (commit 26bb785) driven by `src/fastcore/harness.c` replays #1715M with RAM identical to the FCEUX dump on 17,856 consecutive rows (FCEUX rows 11–17866; one row, 44, differs in 2 OAM-buffer bytes because the post-Start lag frame's NMI spans the frame boundary and is snapshotted at different points), same 24 lag rows, axe on QuickNES row 17865 = FCEUX row 17868. **Alignment law:** FCEUX row r ↔ QuickNES row r−3; fm2 record j is applied by FCEUX in row j+2 (Start: record 41 → row 43) and must be fed to QuickNES frame j−2 (`--input-skip 2`). | V | `docs/experiments/P1.1-fast-core.md`; `tools/compare_ram.py data/wr/fceux_wr.ram runs/qn_wr.ram --offset -3 --from 10 --ignore 160-1ff --all` |
| F46 | **Stage-1 core throughput** (i5-1335U, 12 threads): 15.0k fps per instance on the full WR; savestate 12,792 bytes, serialize+unserialize 2.5 µs; 6 parallel instances 69k fps, 12 → 104k fps aggregate. The libretro build renders every frame (`emulate_skip_frame` unused). | V | `./build/harness … --state-every 1`; `docs/experiments/P1.1-fast-core.md` |
| F47 | **1-1 third room is a fixed puzzle.** The pipe exit puts Mario at X = 2616 (page 10, $38) with subpixel $0705 = 0 and speed 0 (`InitializeArea` clears them); control starts at load+122 (F33). The WR accelerates 0→40 in 33 frames (L+R at control so facing = 3 ≠ moving dir 1, jump: airborne doubled adder to the air cap 24 by frame 1068; land; Left tap → facing 2 ≠ moving 1 → doubled $E4 to 40 by 1079), runs at 40 to x = 3157.3 at frame 1279 (model step 233), is stopped by the pole's base block (X 3158, speed → 0) and lands on the ground beside it (Y 176, step 236), then **jumps** (A+L+R, step 237) and touches the pole two frames later while rising, at X 3161, Y 164 (step 239 = frame 1285; the glitch needs Y ≥ 162, F55). (Session 4 correction from the validated model trace, `smb-opt trace11room`; the earlier "embedded creep" reading of the dump was wrong in mechanism, not in numbers.) Grabs need X & 15 ∈ [6,10) at the pole column → X ∈ [3158, 3161] for column 198. | V | `docs/experiments/P2.1a-bfs-engine.md`; dump `runs/`/`tools/ram_trace.py` rows 1046–1290; smbdis.asm `X_Physics`, `ImposeFriction`, `HandleClimbing`, `BlockBufferCollision` |
| F48 | **Horizontal acceleration mechanics.** Speed ($57) is in 1/16 px/frame; each frame the friction adder (FrictionData $E4 run / $98 walk / $D0 fast-walk) is added to the shared subpixel byte $0705 with carry into speed, **doubled when PlayerFacingDir ≠ Player_MovingDir** (moving dir is refreshed every frame from the speed sign; facing only from ground input); caps: $28 on the ground running (B or RunningTimer, L/R == moving dir) or airborne with speed ≥ 25, else $18 (airborne below 25 cannot exceed 24). Position: $0705 += (speed<<4)&$FF, X += speed>>4 + carry. Sound bound: speed gains ≤ 2/frame; under it the frontier from the third-room entry reaches x = 3158 at frame 1272 (WR: 1280). | V | smbdis.asm lines 6042–6195, 6227–6270, 7536–7575, 5598–5605; `docs/experiments/P2.1a-bfs-engine.md` |
| F49 | **Full-state BFS explosion.** From the third-room load with dedup on RAM ($0000–$00FF, $0300–$07FF): 16 states per layer until control, then 16 → 127 → 936 → 5,402 → 26,247 → 107,757 → >245k in the first 6 control frames (×5/layer, dup ratio 3.5); the sound ≤2/frame bound prunes nothing there (WR is 18.7 px inside it). 12.8 KB savestates cap a layer at ~245k states per 6 GB; expansion costs ~75 µs per child on QuickNES. | V | `src/search/bfs.c`; `docs/experiments/P2.1a-bfs-engine.md` |
| F50 | **Savestate = template + RAM.** In QuickNES's 12,792-byte savestate the 2 KiB system RAM sits at offset 216. Patching a state's RAM into a template savestate taken at another frame (even the title screen, frame 200) and unserializing reproduces the true continuation exactly for 600 frames on the WR (tested from frames 1100, 1229, 1280: through the flagpole sequence and the 1-2 load). In-level the CPU idles between NMIs and the PPU/APU state is a function of RAM, so a search state is the 2 KiB RAM — **except at lag-frame boundaries** (the CPU is mid-NMI, not in RAM): a search must not start at or pass through an area-load lag frame (P2.1b: root 923 collapsed to one state per layer; root ≥ 930 or 1040 is fine). | V | `src/fastcore/state_probe.c` (`build/state_probe … --frame-a 1046 --frame-b 1229 --run 600`) |

| F62 | **Pause and TimerControl law.** While `GamePauseStatus` d0 is set the NMI skips all timers (frame + interval incl. `IntervalTimerControl`), `FrameCounter` and all game logic, but **still steps the LFSR** (`PauseSkip` → `RotPRandomBit`); a pause lasts ≥ 44 frames (`GamePauseTimer` $2B gates the next Start). `TimerControl` ≠ 0 (injury/death $FF) skips the timers but not `FrameCounter` or the LFSR. Select does nothing in play; Start/Select are edge-triggered in `ReadJoypads`. L+R and U+D are never masked: L+R sets facing 3 (doubled friction, never the run cap on the ground, Right wins for acceleration); U+D while climbing = Up; Down on the ground cancels L/R (crouch only when big). A jump needs a fresh A press; the jump velocity uses `Player_XSpeedAbsolute`, updated only by `ImposeFriction`. | V | `docs/input-semantics.md` (smbdis.asm 780–812, 851–882, 2421–2447, 5558–5590, 5903–5952, 5966–6062, 6064–6130, 6139–6190, 6227–6270) |

| F95 | **Hetzner Cloud billing and the account quota.** *Quota (2026-08-22): the project account is new and only the three lowest dedicated tiers (ccx13/ccx23/ccx33, ≤ 8 cores) are offered; ccx43/53/63 need a limits increase and the console refuses the request as the account is too new. Shared-tier availability unconfirmed.* Hourly, rounded up (a partial hour bills as one; no daily minimum), and charged as the *lower* of hours × hourly or the monthly cap (ccx63: 744 h × $1.6138 = $1,200.67 → capped at $1,006.99). **A powered-off server still bills — "we will bill you for your servers until you delete them, independent of their state"; only `hcloud server delete` stops the charge.** Dedicated x86 rates (fsn1/nbg1/hel1, USD net): ccx33 8c/32 GB/240 GB $0.2612/h, ccx43 16c/64 GB/360 GB $0.5216/h, ccx53 32c/128 GB/600 GB $1.0088/h, ccx63 48c/192 GB/960 GB $1.6138/h — **$/core-hour is flat to within 6 %** (0.0315–0.0336), so the $300 cap is ~9,000 dedicated-core-hours and machine size buys wall-clock only. Ancillaries: snapshots $0.0199/GB-month (compressed size), volumes $0.0767/GB-month (billed while they exist), primary IPv4 $0.60/month billed separately (IPv6 free), automatic backups a flat 20 % of the server price. **Shared tiers are 4-10x cheaper per core** (cx53 16c/32 GB/320 GB $0.0561/h = $0.0035/core-h, cax41 ARM 16c $0.0777/h, cpx51 16c $0.1338/h) but are sold as baseline-plus-burst, not sustained full load; Hetzner's FAQ neither permits nor forbids sustained 100 % use, so contention and tolerance are **unmeasured**. | V | <https://docs.hetzner.com/cloud/billing/faq/>; authenticated `GET https://api.hetzner.cloud/v1/pricing`; `hcloud server-type describe ccx{33,43,53,63}`; `docs/experiments/P0.10-cloud-sizing.md` §2–3 |
| F96 | **`bfscx`/`bfs11c` scaling limit is the serial merge.** Expansion is embarrassingly parallel over `--threads N` (each thread slices the parent layer file, expands 16 inputs/parent, sorts/dedups a private `--acc-mb` accumulator, spills sorted runs); the k-way merge that writes the next layer file is a **single-threaded** `BinaryHeap` drain (`third_party/smb-opt/src/main.rs`, expansion ~1165, merge ~1272). Measured on run 4's ~700 M-state layers (i5-1335U, `--threads 12`, `--acc-mb 256`): expand 978–1401 s vs merge 36.6–75.3 s of 43–49 runs = **serial fraction 4–6 %**, i.e. ~14,400 laptop-core-seconds of parallel work per big layer. Amdahl (assuming a Hetzner dedicated core ≈ 1.6× an i5-1335U E-core — **unmeasured**): 16c ≈ 2.1×, 32c ≈ 3.8×, 48c ≈ 5.4× (merge 21 % of wall), 64c ≈ 6.7× (26 %) → **the knee is ~48 cores** until the merge is parallelised (P2.3c-3: bucket run output by key prefix, merge buckets independently, concatenate in key order). Raising `--acc-mb` (192 GB allows ~3000 MB × 48 threads) cuts the run count and shrinks the merge without code changes. | V | `runs/P2.1b-model/room_compact_d238_r85.log` layers 137/141; `third_party/smb-opt/src/main.rs`; `docs/experiments/P0.10-cloud-sizing.md` §4 |
| F97 | **Hetzner dedicated / Server Auction terms and day rates** (the route around the Cloud quota; Robot is a separate product with no vCPU-quota system). **No setup fee** on the auction (*"There are no setup fees for these servers"*), **no minimum contract term**, and Robot lets you *"usually choose to cancel the server immediately"* with no notice period. **Billing is exact to the hour, pro-rata, charged after use — "you will never pay more than the monthly price"** ⇒ the monthly figure is a *ceiling*, not a commitment, so compare **day rates**. Hetzner's "30 days to the end of the month" policy does not bite (Cloud exempt; dedicated cancellable immediately; even under it you lose access at once and pay only for time held). New AX-line servers are worse value: setup fees rose in April 2026 (AX102 €79 → €269) and the 15 June 2026 restructure raised monthly prices — **the auction was explicitly excluded from both**. Live 2026-08-22 (net, incl. €1.70 IPv4): EPYC 7502P 32c/64t 128 GB + 7.7 TB €333.70/mo (~$12.10/day) or + 1.9 TB €266.70 (~$9.67/day); Ryzen 9 5950X 16c/32t 128 GB + 7.7 TB €247.70; Ryzen 9 3900 12c/24t 128 GB + 3.8 TB €178.70 (~$6.48/day); Ryzen 7 7700 8c/16t 64 GB + 2 TB €105.70. **Against the cloud ccx33 (8c/32 GB/240 GB, $6.27/day), the 32c/64t EPYC with 1.9 TB is $9.67/day — 1.5× the price for 4× the cores, 4× the RAM, 8× the disk.** Caveats: provisioning up to one business day and **new accounts may be delayed by verification** (the account-age risk can reappear here — unverified for this account); used, unmanaged hardware, no stated warranty; inventory rotates fast. | V | <https://docs.hetzner.com/robot/general/server-auction-faqs/>; <https://docs.hetzner.com/general/billing-and-account-management/cancellation/cancellations-robot/>; <https://docs.hetzner.com/general/general-terms-and-conditions/30-days-to-the-end-of-the-month/>; <https://www.hetzner.com/pressroom/standardization-and-price-adjustment-of-our-server-products/>; Server Radar (`radar.iodev.org`, browser UA required); `docs/experiments/P0.10-cloud-sizing.md` §8 |
| F98 | **4-2 main-area search size is set by the bound's slack, and a tight bound keeps the frontier flat.** Measured on the S1 segment ladder (`runs/P2.3c/top_s1_x339_y112_d149.log`, 4-2 main area, **2 threads**, deadline 149 = 2 frames above the 147 that has no path): the frontier **plateaus at ~1.4×10⁷ and turns over** — 13.7M, 14.0M, 14.1M, 14.1M, 14.0M, 13.9M at layers 85–90 — at ~131 s/layer, 90 layers in 41 minutes. Contrast the warp zone at 15 frames of slack: 9.2M at layer 24 growing ×1.25/layer (F91), and run 4's ~7×10⁸/layer at ~1000 s. **Machine sizing therefore spans three orders of magnitude on this one number:** at ~10⁷/layer the full 575-layer run is ~4–8 h on the laptop (~190 GB raw, ~7 GB xz at 24 B/record) and needs nothing rented; at ~10⁸ it is ~2 days; at ~10⁹ it is ~3 weeks and wants the auction EPYC (F97). Caveat: S1 is a *segment* search with a fixed position goal, so the full-level frontier will be larger — the mechanism is measured, the magnitude is not. **Do not rent on spec**: a box ordered before the bound lands idles at $10–12/day. | V | `runs/P2.3c/top_s1_x339_y112_d149.log` layers 85–90; `runs/P2.3c/top_s1_x339_y112_d147.log` (no path, layer 29); `docs/experiments/P0.10-cloud-sizing.md` §9 |

| F100 | **Piranha plants are spawned by the area parser, one per vertical pipe, into the first free enemy slot 0–4 — and there is no "no plant" bit.** `VerticalPipe` (`smbdis.asm` 3843–3884) spawns a plant when it renders the pipe's *left* column unless `AreaNumber|WorldNumber == 0` (1-1) or no slot is free (then the pipe is drawn without one). In 4-2 main: pipe A (col 72) row 7004, pipe B (col 78) row 7042, pipe C (col 89) row 7110 — each on the frame whose parser task is a render task (post-frame `AreaParserTaskNum` 7 or 3) with `CurrentPageLoc·16 + CurrentColumnPos` = the pipe's column; **the wrong-warp pipe (col 84, row 7080) got no plant in the WR only because all five slots were occupied** (plant A, the vine, plant B, the lift, the koopa) — a route that frees a slot (no vine bump, an erased lift, a stomped goomba …) gets a plant in the wrong-warp pipe. Initial RAM: x = col·16 + 8, y = row·16 + 32 (= the pipe top), `PiranhaPlant_Y_Speed` (= Enemy_X_Speed) 1, `MoveFlag` (= Enemy_Y_Speed) 0, DownYPos = y, UpYPos = y − 24, bbox ctrl 9 (x+3..x+13, y+14..y+20); frame timer / collision bits / moving dir inherited from the slot. Movement (`MovePiranhaPlant` 10577–10634): 1 px on odd `FrameCounter` frames, 24 px of travel, then `EnemyFrameTimer` = $40 (63 idle frames; frame timers tick every frame while `TimerControl` = 0); parked at the top it descends unconditionally when the timer expires; parked at the bottom it rises only when the low byte of \|enemy x − Mario x\| (sign from the 16-bit difference) is ≥ $21 — **this distance rule is asm-sourced, the WR never exercises it** (control: threshold $41 → 0 mismatches). Contact = injury/death, no stomp (11338–11339); excluded from enemy–enemy collision; never erased on the right, erased on the left at ScreenLeft − 16 (−271 while ScreenLeft_X ≥ $c7). The dump: A rises 160 → 136 over rows 7005–7051, waits to 7115, descends; B is fully up (y 72) when the WR jumps pipe B (~7150); A erased at 7154. | V (spawn/cycle/erase on the dump) + S (distance rule: asm) | `tools/w42_enemy_sim.py`; `docs/experiments/P2.5c-notes-piranha.md` (line refs); `P2.5c-w42-enemies.md` |
| F101 | **The player's hitbox for enemy collisions is built from Mario's position *before* `PlayerBGCollision`.** In `PlayerCtrlRoutine` the order is `PlayerMovementSubs` → `ScrollHandler` → `RelativePlayerPosition` → `BoundingBoxCore` (5607–5609) → `PlayerBGCollision` (5610); the enemies' `PlayerEnemyCollision` (run later in the frame) compares the enemy box with that box. So a landing snap (`LandPlyr`: Y 129 → 128) or a wall push-back (`ImpedePlayerMove`: x 1235 → 1234) in the same frame does not move the box: Mario landing on pipe A at Y 128 is killed by the rising plant whose box starts at 161 (his box is 149..161, from Y 129 — RAM `$04AC..$04AF` in the dump), and Mario pressing into pipe B is missed by a koopa whose box ends at 134 (his box is 135..145, from x 1235). The box arithmetic itself is the closed-interval test (`SprObjectCollisionCore` 12934–12990, no wrap): `BoundBoxCtrlData` entry 1 (small Mario) = (3,20,13,32), entry 9 (plant) = (3,14,13,20), entry 3 (koopa/beetle) = (2,9,14,21). | F120 | **The scroll-offset latch, and the wrong warp's hidden requirement.** (1) SMB1's screen scrolls by the player's movement-dx once his screen-relative x reaches 112, tracks it exactly (no catch-up), and never scrolls left: the relative offset is a latch that persists until changed. (2) Collision-push displacement (`ImpedePlayerMove` in the moving direction, the F93 foot-check drift) moves the player WITHOUT feeding the scroll: it is the only known offset-minting mechanism. The WR mints +21 px (relative 111 → 132) during its col-30 wall entry, rows 6786–6819, with ScreenLeft frozen at 357; our top-route run stays at 112 from x 160 to the end (dump scan of every offset change, both FCEUX dumps). (3) The 4-2 wrong warp therefore requires ~20 px of minted offset: entry at x ∈ [1348, 1356] with the (page-5, col-15) area-change command unparsed needs ScreenLeft ≤ 1216 ⇔ relative ≥ 132 (F40; parse threshold: SL ≥ 1217 puts the command inside ScreenRight+48 masked to 16). Slack cannot mint offset (hang-back stops the scroll too; the constrained rungs d44–49 died exactly at max x 1328.94 = relative 112). **HappyLee's wall walk is passage + vine + warp key at once.** | V | the dump scan in `P2.3c-2c-main-area.md` §"The scroll-offset discovery"; `runs/P2.3c-2c/s4w4_entry_d4*.log`; F40/F93 |
| F121 | **The x-only case bound is why the drift rungs blew up, and the scroll-aware drift bound is sound.** The two dedicated wrong-warp rungs (local d58, Mac d62) were watchdog-killed at layer 37 (frontier 126M / 249M, `max x 0x530f0` = 1328.94 = the F40 refusal wall, `goals 0`, growth ×1.5–1.6/layer) — memory blowups, **not** verdicts: `W42Main`'s `--enemies` case bound was the x-only table to X 1348, blind to the F120 scroll requirement, so every wall-pinned state looked ~2 frames from the goal and survived. `heuristics::drift::DriftBound` (a relaxed BFS over X∈[1180,1360]×Player_Y×offset m∈[−40,24]×side-collision credit c∈[0,16], transitions = the exact scroll regimes + collision pushes at BB42 sites, distance from X≥1348 within the `X ≤ 1328+m` wall) is an admissible lower bound: control gate byte-identical with drift off (`6 16 34 70 134 673 3472 16472 69489 257001`); the WR's real warp survives `--check-path` with 0 violations and reaches the goal (drift ON), while 32,472 wrong-scroll pipe entries are now pruned (`goal_refused`); `tools/drift_audit.py` — 400 trajectories + WR, 211,675 consistency pairs `h(s)≤h(s′)+1`, **0 violations**, minting exercised to rel 132, `h` up to 67 in the wall band (vs the x-only ~2). Case bound now `max(x-table, drift)`, ON by default under `--enemies`, `--no-drift` to disable. | V | `docs/experiments/P2.3c-2c-main-area.md` §P2.3c-5; `third_party/smb-opt/src/heuristics/drift.rs`; `tools/drift_audit.py`; `runs/P2.3c-2c/s4w4_d58_drift.log` |
| F122 | **The 4-2 top route cannot mint the scroll offset the wrong warp requires — strong evidence the top-route warp is infeasible (not a 2-frame budget miss).** The warp needs offset (x − ScreenLeft) ≥ 132 at entry (WR: exactly 132; parse threshold asm-exact at SL ≥ 1217, `CheckRightBounds`); the top route reaches the pipe at offset **112** (F118's 553). The only minting mechanism is collision-push (`impede_player_move`: moving-dir LEFT + speed 0 + a foot/side point in a solid cell → the col-30 **floor-level** wall walk, F93/F120). Across **five** searches — `bfscx --beam` h-first & offset-first from 509 (d72) and 484 (d100), and two no-beam exhaustive probes (drift from step 530 d35; position-goal 484→x1284 d55, 23 layers exhaustive over x 1177→1234) — the offset **never leaves 112**. Mechanistic reason: the top route's walls are top surfaces (bricks) or jumped-over (pipe B), never a floor-level face in a mint pose, and **F117** shows it cannot reach floor level past pipe A. | F128 | **A single-key beam is a per-layer first-arrival gate — the search method itself was losing solutions (H39 confirmed, with F127 as the casualty).** `bfscx --beam N` (lowest-h) and `--beam-offset` (highest-offset, then h) are both *global single-key* orders, so any maneuver that must **pay before it gains** — slow down, back off in x, drop height — has worsening key on exactly the layers it must survive and is deleted before it can pay. That is the same lossy operation as a first-arrival segment seam, applied every frame rather than every seam: a beam does not escape segment-greedy search, it *is* segment-greedy search at 1-frame granularity. Demonstrated: F127's mint requires **19 consecutive frames of held Left**, which h-first prunes on frame 1; under the same root/deadline/budget a bucketed beam finds it and a global one does not. **Fix shipped:** `bfscx --beam N --beam-buckets off,y,spd,sub[,vf] [--beam-max M]` — best N *per physical bucket* (scroll offset, 16 px y band, x-speed band, x subpixel phase (F11/F37), v_force); `--beam-max` shrinks the per-bucket width first and only drops whole buckets as a last resort, printing `WARNING capped` when it does. Also `smb-opt offset-census CASE LAYER_FILE [--top K] [--min N]` — `tools/pick_parent.py` cannot decode `left_screen_edge_pos`, so the scroll offset (the quantity F122/F123 turn on) was invisible to the seam census until now. **Regression: beam-off is byte-identical** — the `--lift 0` control gate still gives 6, 16, 34, 70, 134, 673, 3472, 16472, 69489, 257001 (`runs/P2.3c-8/gate_beamoff.log`). Corollary for seams generally: the runbook's step-3 "reconstruct TWO candidates differing in vertical situation" is this same insight applied by hand at width 2; every first-arrival segment gate in the project carries the same risk. | V (A/B search record + regression gate) | `runs/P2.3c-8/{gate_beamoff.log,probe10.log,f122_retest.log}`; `third_party/smb-opt/src/main.rs` (`parse_beam_buckets`, `bucket_key`, `offset_census`); `docs/experiments/P2.3c-8-beam-diversity.md`; H39 |

| F130 | **The 8-4 flying-cheep frenzy couples to the player far more weakly than F126 assumed: speed enters only as a 3-valued class, and position is an additive spawn base, not a branch — so the `w84enemies` port is much cheaper than budgeted.** Read at code level from `InitFlyingCheepCheep` (`data/disasm/smbdis.asm:8419-8500`). **(a) Speed → coarse classes only.** The seed path is `lda #$00 / ldy Player_X_Speed / beq GSeed` (A=0) `/ lda #$04 / cpy #$19 / bcc GSeed` (A=4) `/ asl` (A=8), then `adc $01` with `$01` = `PseudoRandomBitReg,x & 3` → `tay` → `lda FlyCCXSpeedData,y` (12 entries). Absolute player X never selects a branch, so it does **not** need to be a bucket/key dimension — the spawn geometry is translation-invariant. **(c) What is left as real state:** `FrenzyEnemyTimer` (gates the whole routine; reloaded from `FlyCCTimerData[LFSR+1 & 3]`), the enemy-slot count test `cpx $00` (slot occupancy), and the LFSR itself — which on a lag-free route is a pure function of the frame. Note `$00` is **overwritten** with `PseudoRandomBitReg+2,x & $0f` whenever `PseudoRandomBitReg+1,x & 3 != 0`, so the *position* index is usually pure LFSR with no speed dependence at all. `SecondaryHardMode` (max 3 vs 4 onscreen) is off on this route. **Consequence:** F126's "NOT frame-indexed ⇒ materially bigger than budgeted" stands in direction but is quantitatively far too pessimistic — the frenzy is frame-indexed *up to a 3-valued speed class*, with player-relative spawns. | F137 | **8-4 room 2's x-only bound cannot carry the tail, and that alone makes a beam useless there.** The clip pipe's entry window starts at x 2436, which the frontier reaches *at floor level* about 15 frames before the goal; from there `h = 0` for every state and the search has no signal left for the two-stage climb the entry needs (wedge against the pipe's left face -> stand at Y 96 on its row-6 body -> jump to the cap at Y 64 -> walk right to x 2436). Measured: a bucketed beam at the WR's OWN deadline 267 finds **no goal** at width 50 (70 s) and again at width 2000 with ~300k states/layer (395 s), both racing past the window to x 2459-2471 at floor level. The fix is a y-coupled bound over the goal's own **necessary** condition `x >= 2436 && Player_Y <= 64` (the entry can only fire standing on the cap): `YGate` over `surfaces_from_blocks::<BB84>(304)`, joined with the x table. **Trap**: `YGate::steps_xy` is a "steps until it holds in the FUTURE" bound whose scan starts at k = 1, so it never returns 0 and over-counts by 1 on a state that already satisfies the condition — which the goal state does, so using it raw prunes the WR's own goal (`--check-path`: `h Some(1)` at the GOAL step, 1 bound violation). States already satisfying the condition must be given h = 0 explicitly. With that, the WR line audits clean (0 bound violations over 267 steps, h = 0 at the goal) and `tools/ygate_audit.py --mutate` gives **1335 admissibility checks, 0 violations, min slack 0** (tight at the goal, no `Y_NEVER` on any goal path). | V (two beam controls + the reference-path audit + the mutation audit) | `runs/P2.2f/d267_beam{,_w2000}.log`, `d267_ygate_w200.log`; `third_party/smb-opt/src/case/w84.rs` (`W84Room2::distance_to_goal_heuristic`, `W84R2_PIPE_Y`) |

That slack is the part of the tail the bound cannot see (Mario must LAND on the pipe's left face before he can jump to the cap; the gate prices an immediate jump), so an h-first order prefers, on every layer, the family that is *ahead* of the WR on x — and the WR's family is behind by construction. | F141 | **1-2's main area is now modelled EXACTLY end to end — 1280/1280 frames — and finishing it exposed two real bugs in the shared enemy engine.** The port needed two new classes. **`CLASS_LIFTPLAT`** covers ids `$26` and `$27`, which `LargePlatformSubroutines` both dispatch to `MoveLargeLiftPlat`: motion is `YMF_Dummy += Y_MoveForce; Y_Position += Y_Speed + carry` (10982) with Y wrapping in the byte and `Enemy_Y_HighPos` untouched; init is `PlatLiftDown` ($27) force `$f0`/speed `$00` or `PlatLiftUp` ($26) `$10`/`$ff`, then `PosPlatform` with Y = 1 which adds **12 px to the X, not the Y**; Mario meets it via `LargePlatformCollision` + `PositionPlayerOnVPlat`, never `PlayerEnemyCollision`. **4-2's lift is the same object** — its bespoke `lift_y_after(k) = Y0 + m - m/16` is the closed form of that recurrence — so its validated collision code was reused. **`CLASS_REDKOOPA`** ($03) is `InitNormalEnemy` + `Enemy_State = 1`, plus `ChkForRedKoopa` (12550): nothing underneath and state 0 means turn around and `rts` — no state update, no side check. **BUG 1: `CheckpointEnemyID`'s +8 px (and `EnemyOffscrBitsMasked = 1`) apply only to ids < `$15`** — they sit after `cmp #$15 / bcs InitEnemyRoutines`, and the ROM comments them "($00-$14 only)". The model applied both unconditionally, putting every platform, powerup and vine **8 px too low**. **BUG 2 (live in 4-2 and 8-4 as well): bounding boxes were never clamped at the screen edges.** Boxes are screen-RELATIVE bytes, so one near an edge wraps; `CheckRightScreenBBox`/`CheckLeftScreenBBox` (12891) push such a box fully offscreen ($ff right / $00 left). Without it **two goombas 250 px apart turned each other around** — 18 of 150 battery trials, every one a phantom stomp. **Validation after both fixes:** WR 1280/1280 with 6 stomps and the pipe entry frame-identical; 1-2 battery **150 trials / 96,424 frames / 0 differences** (203 stomps, 24 kicks, 102 matching deaths); and every prior regression still exact — 4-2's control gate, 4-2's 596-frame enemy chain, and 8-4 room 2 at 401 trials / 0 differences. | V (core difftest, battery, and three regressions) | `third_party/smb-opt/src/w42enemies.rs` (`CLASS_LIFTPLAT`, `CLASS_REDKOOPA`, `clamp_bbox`, `init_enemy`); `runs/P2.3e/batt_seed5.log`; `docs/experiments/P2.3e-framerule-scan.md` |

| F143 | **1-2's bound slack is all in one place — the wall clip — and probing it produced a textbook first-arrival-gate near-miss plus a second optimality proof.** The `--check-path` slack profile is flat at **55 from step 40 to ~500 and 54 to ~1080** (Mario is at the x-speed cap 40 for essentially the whole level, so bound and truth advance in lockstep), then collapses **54 -> 23 between steps 1080 and 1120**: the WR spends 40 frames where the bound expects 9. | F144 | **THREE FRAMES GENUINELY EXIST IN 1-2's WALL CLIP — and the scroll takes them back, because 1-2's warp zone arms on SCREEN LEFT, not on Mario.** An exhaustive rung from the WR's step-1080 state to `x >= 2784 && Y <= 64` (the post-clip milestone) **reaches it at layer 77 where the WR pays 80** (2,016,915 goal transitions, 470 s), and unlike F143's near-miss the arrival **dominates** the WR's: x_pos 0xae130 vs 0xae0f0, x_spd 0x28fc vs 0x28d8 (both at the cap 40, ours with the better fraction), identical y_pos 0x140b0 / STANDING / running_speed / running_timer 10. **Core-verified 77/77 frames, 0 mismatches.** Continuing: from that state the WR's step-1220 milestone (`x >= 2933 && Y <= 128`) takes **exactly 60** frames with `h(root) = 60 = deadline`, so 60 is provably minimal — putting us at step **1217, still 3 ahead**, and the whole 137-frame segment core-verifies at **0 mismatches**. **Then it dies: from step 1217 the warp pipe is DRY at 60**, the very cost the WR pays from its step-1220 state. **The mechanism:** `ScrollLockObject_Warp` (smbdis.asm:3566) stores `WarpZoneControl = 4` when ScreenLeft reaches its locked maximum **2816** — a function of where Mario has BEEN. | F146 | **8-3's Hammer Bro is not an RNG blocker — the LFSR is frame-indexed and the Bro's three uses of it price out to nothing.** `PseudoRandomBitReg` is advanced at `PauseSkip` in the NMI timer block right after `inc FrameCounter`, as an unconditional 7-byte rotate with feedback from d1 of bytes 0 and 1 (smbdis.asm:802-815) — **no player input enters it**, so on a lag-free route the whole register is a pure function of the frame count and can be precomputed per step (the same shape F130 established for the cheep frenzy's timer). `ProcHammerBro` (9212) reads it in exactly three places: `SpawnHammerObj`'s `PseudoRandomBitReg+1 & 7` (which misc slot the hammer takes, hence whether the throw succeeds) — frame-indexed; `HammerBroJumpCode`'s `PseudoRandomBitReg+1,x & 1` (vertical speed `$fa` vs `$fd`, and only while the Bro is below mid-screen) — frame-indexed and 2-valued; and the jump-length offset `$00 & PseudoRandomBitReg+2,x`, which is **inert in normal play** because the offset is only consulted when `SecondaryHardMode` is set (`bne HJump`, else `tay` forces 0), so the jump length is always `HammerBroJumpLData[0] = $20`. **So 8-3's standing blocker — "Hammer Bros are RNG-driven and the model has no RNG" — dissolves the way F126's did**, subject to a difftest confirming it. **4-1's Lakitu (`$11`) is the harder one** and stays unpriced: it reads the same frame-indexed register but also tracks Mario's position continuously, so its coupling is to the trajectory, not the frame index. **Queue consequence: 8-3 leads 4-1 on all three axes** — deficit 7 vs 9, no new block map needed, cheaper RNG object. | V (code-level derivation) | `data/disasm/smbdis.asm` 802-815 (`PauseSkip`/`RotPRandomBit`), 9212-9270 (`ProcHammerBro`/`HammerBroJumpCode`), 6873-6896 (`SpawnHammerObj`); `docs/experiments/P2.3e-framerule-scan.md` §22 |
| F147 | **A silent 1-frame parser error in 1-2's model, caused by `IgnoreCoins` — collecting a coin STALLS the area parser, and the stall moves piranha-plant spawns.** Found the only way it could be: a search result core-replayed and **the core killed Mario while the model did not** (`replay_check` -> `GES 11` at frame 3690, `death (False, True)`). Cause chain, traced end to end: `AwardTouchedCoin` fills `VRAM_Buffer1`; a non-empty VRAM buffer makes the frame's area-parser task not run (`AreaParserTaskNum` repeats, core sequence `… 6, 5, **5**, 4 …` against the model's `… 6, 5, 4 …`); the parser column therefore advances a frame late; and the piranha plant the parser spawns at that column appears a frame late too. Over the WR's own 1280 frames the model spawned the pipe-(103,8) plant at f3058 where the core spawns it at **f3059**, and by the warp zone that had become a **1 px** offset in a plant's y — the model's plant box at y 143 versus the core's 142, with Mario's box bottom at 156 exactly touching the core's plant top and missing the model's. **1 px of plant, and the difference between a valid path and a death.** The WR difftest never saw it (1280/1280 clean) because the WR never touches that plant; it only bites on searched paths. **Fix:** `coin_list_handler!` moved from `w42.rs` to `case/mod.rs` (it was declared after `w12` in the module list, so 1-2 could not see it) and `W12Warp` given its own 17-coin handler over `BB12W`'s `$c2` cells; key 20 -> 22 bytes. After the fix the model's parser stalls on exactly the core's frame and the plant spawns at f3059. **Re-verified on the fixed model:** WR 1280/1280; the loss map unchanged (66 frames, bound tight from step 1234); F142's rungs dry at layers 4 and 14 and F143's at 16 — the same layer numbers as before; and 4-2's control gate, 4-2's 596-frame chain and 8-4 room 2 all unchanged. **8-4 room 2 needs no coin handler** — its span has no `$c2` cell. | V (core replay disagreement, traced to the parser schedule and fixed) | `third_party/smb-opt/src/case/{mod.rs,w12.rs,w42.rs}`, `state.rs` (`[u8; 22]`); `runs/P2.3e/wr_hprofile2.txt` |
| F148 | **A residual parser-ordering gap in the model: a same-frame coin award cannot stall the same frame's parser, and every 1-2 search result is provisional until it is fixed.** After F147's coin handler the corrected clip search reproduced itself exactly (goal at layer 78, 3,180,666 transitions; carry at layer 60, 7,879,141) and the 78-frame clip core-verified 78/78 — **and the core still killed Mario at frame 3690**, same warp-zone plant, same 1 px. The pipe-(103,8) plant F147 fixed now spawns at f3059 in both, but the **warp-zone pipe-(182,8) plant at x 2920 spawns f3566 in the model against the core's f3567**, and that is true **on the WR's own path**. Walking `AreaParserTaskNum` frame by frame against the core over the WR's whole line, the first divergence is **step 446 / f2930**: the core stalls (task number stays 3), the model advances (3 -> 2), and on that exact frame `CoinTally` goes 20 -> 21 with `VRAM_Buffer1_Offset` = 31 — Mario head-bumps the coin at **(68, 6)**, which IS in `W12WarpCoins`, so the model collects it and calls `note_coin_award`. **CORRECTED by F149 — the ordering diagnosis recorded here was wrong.** `run_step` already calls `player_ctrl_routine()` **before** `block_states_game_engine()`, so a same-frame award does stall the same frame's parser; the order matches `GameEngine`. The real cause was that the model never awarded that coin at all: Mario head-bumps the **brick at (68, 7)** and the `$c2` at (68, 6) is the coin **above** it, taken by `CheckTopOfBlock`, which the model did not implement (F149). **Consequences:** the 2-frame 1-2 candidate is NOT verified (the core rejects it and is right), and F142/F143's dry rungs carried this gap in both runs, so they are provisional too. **Unaffected:** the WR-line difftest (the WR never touches the mis-timed plants), the bound and its loss map, 4-2 and 8-4 (4-2's slip changes nothing its difftest compares; 8-4 room 2 has no coins), and the structural facts of F144/F145. **Acceptance test (kept, and it is the right one):** require **frame-by-frame `AreaParserTaskNum` equality with the core over the WR's 1280 frames** — a far stronger control than the field difftest, and the one that should have been run when the case was built. | V (frame-by-frame parser walk vs the core, plus two core replays) | `runs/P2.3e/dbg2/`; `third_party/smb-opt/src/emu.rs` (`run_step`:223, `block_states_game_engine`:170, `note_coin_award`:146, `player_bg_collision`:441) |
| F149 | **Bumping a block awards the `$c2` coin sitting directly above it (`CheckTopOfBlock`) — the model did not, and that was F148's real cause.** `BumpBlock` and `BrickShatter` both open with `jsr CheckTopOfBlock` (smbdis.asm:7307, 7380), which reads the block buffer one row **up** from the bumped cell, and if it is exactly `$c2` erases it, calls `RemoveCoin_Axe` (which sets `VRAM_Buffer_AddrCtrl = 6` and therefore **stalls the area parser for the frame**) and `SetupJumpCoin` (which increments `CoinTally`). In 1-2 exactly two cells on the WR's route are hit this way — the plain bricks at **(60, 7)** and **(68, 7)**, each with a `$c2` at (60, 6) / (68, 6) — confirmed against the core: at row 2887 a block object spawns in slot 1 at abs X 960 / Y 142 (= cell 60, row 7) with `CoinTally` 19 -> 20 and `VRAM_Buffer_AddrCtrl` 6, and at row 2934 the same in slot 0 at abs X 1088 (= cell 68, row 7) with 20 -> 21. **`SetupJumpCoin` has exactly one caller in the whole ROM** (`CheckTopOfBlock`), so this is the only bump-awards-a-coin path that touches VRAM. The other one, **`CoinBlock`** (bumping a `$c0`/`$5f`/`$58`/`$5d` whose *contents* is a coin, reached from `BumpBlock`'s `BlockBumpedChk` -> `BlockCode` jump table), calls `GiveOneCoin` **without** `RemoveCoin_Axe`, so it moves `CoinTally` but writes only status-bar digits and **cannot stall the parser** — the model is right to ignore it, and 4-2's `$c0` at column 51 is exactly such a block (its missing coin costs nothing). **Fix:** `SmbEmu::check_top_of_block(cx, cy)`, called first in both the `BumpBlock` and the `BrickShatter` branch of `player_bg_collision`. | V (disassembly + core RAM at both bumps; the fix is what took 1-2's parser control to 1280/1280) | `data/disasm/smbdis.asm` 7307/7380/7395-7411 (`CheckTopOfBlock`), 6963-6999 (`CoinBlock`/`SetupJumpCoin`/`GiveOneCoin`), 7361-7367 (`BrickQBlockMetatiles`); `third_party/smb-opt/src/emu.rs` (`check_top_of_block`); `tools/parser_check.py` |
| F150 | **1-2's warp zone runs on `ScrollLock`, which the model did not have — and without it the model's screen-left ran 2 px ahead of the core for the last 222 frames of the level, i.e. up to a phantom frame at the goal.** Three objects, all confirmed against the core: (1) `L_UndergroundArea1` +148 puts a row-13 **`ScrollLockObject`** on page 11 col 2 = **column 178**; when the area parser's `AreaParserCore` (tasks 7 and 3) reaches that column it **toggles** `ScrollLock` (`eor #1`), and `ScrollHandler`'s first test is `lda ScrollLock / bne InitScrlAmt` — a set flag means the screen does not scroll **at all** that frame. (2) `E_UndergroundArea1` +42 puts the **`WarpZoneObject`** (`$34`) on page 11 col 2; it idles until `ScrollLock` is set, then clears it, increments `WarpZoneControl` and erases itself — **but only on a frame where `Player_Y_Position AND Player_Y_HighPos` is zero**. Underground `Player_Y_HighPos` is 1, so **the lock is held for as long as Mario's Y is odd**, and every held frame is a frame of scroll lost. (3) `L_UndergroundArea1` +160 puts **`ScrollLockObject_Warp`** on page 12 col 6 = **column 198**; it sets `WarpZoneControl` (4 for world 1), kills every piranha plant, and then **falls straight through into `ScrollLockObject`** — there is no `rts` between them (smbdis.asm:3566-3585) — so it toggles the lock too, which is why the scroll is locked for the rest of the level. | F152 | **The scroll refund is a one-pixel-per-frame tax, and exactly which frames pay it is known: `ScrollHandler` gives the screen one pixel LESS than Mario moved on every frame his screen position is in [80, 112) — and the full amount once it is >= 112.** The code (`ScrollHandler` -> `ChkNearMid` -> `ScrollScreen`): after the three bail-outs (`ScrollLock` set, `Player_Pos_ForScroll < $50`, `SideCollisionTimer` running) it loads `Y = Player_X_Scroll`, leaves it alone if the value is 0 or 1, otherwise **decrements it**; then `lda Player_Pos_ForScroll / cmp #$70 / bcc ScrollScreen` — below 112 the screen moves by the **decremented** Y, at or above 112 it reloads the **undecremented** `Player_X_Scroll`. So: **ScreenLeft gained = (Mario's rightward displacement) − (one per frame spent in the 80-112 band at >= 2 px/frame) − (everything moved while below 80, or while the lock is set, or while `SideCollisionTimer` runs).** This is the mechanism behind §17's refund and it makes it quantitative rather than mysterious: 1-2's wall clip moves Mario **left**, and leftward motion scrolls nothing (`dey / bmi InitScrlAmt`), so it drops his screen position into the taxed band and the way back out costs one pixel a frame. Arriving at the post-clip milestone **three frames early left the screen three pixels behind** (ours 2806 at step 1217 against the WR's 2809 at 1220) — three frames' worth of tax, not a coincidence. **Consequences:** (a) the scroll is a *lagged follower*, so in 1-2 — where the warp zone arms on `ScreenLeft` reaching 2816 — a frame banked before the warp zone really is a frame lent to the scroll, unless the route keeps Mario at screen position >= 112 through the maneuver; (b) this is an **optimisable quantity with a computable ceiling**, i.e. the shape of the coupled bound term 1-2's search is missing (the analogue of `P2.2f-bound` for 8-4 room 2), because the maximum scroll reachable in k frames from a given (x, screen position) is a function of how few frames the route can spend in the taxed band. | V (disassembly, and it reproduces §17's measured 3 px exactly) | `data/disasm/smbdis.asm` `ScrollHandler`/`ChkNearMid`/`ScrollScreen`; `docs/experiments/P2.3e-framerule-scan.md` §17-18, §34 |
| F153 | **Every stuck search in this project is stuck on the same thing: the x bound does not know about walls. Three separate blockages, one cause, and each one's slack is localised to a handful of frames.** Measured from `--check-path`'s per-step `h` (which is a free loss map): **8-4 room 2** loses **15** frames over 267 and **13 of them are in the three-step window 246-248** — at steps 242-245 Mario is airborne at Y 96 carrying **x_spd 38.97**, and the y gate charges the Y-96 surface with `t_surf` computed from a **running** jump (T = 6), so it reports h = 8 where the truth is 22; on step 246 he lands and his speed collapses to **0.97** because he has hit the pipe's left wall face and `impede_player_move` zeroed it, and h jumps 8 -> 17. From 246 on the bound is nearly tight (17/21, 18/20, 18/19, 17/18). **1-2's joint segment** loses **54** frames over 200, and **51** of them are in two windows: steps 5-49 (~30) where Mario is *inside* the wall with his speed reset every frame — 11 px in 19 frames where the table prices 40 px/frame — and steps 138-154 (~21) where the turnaround is taken airborne off a warp-pipe cap. The 88 frames of open running between them lose **one**. **So the x table is exact in open ground and wrong exactly where terrain touches Mario horizontally.** The consequence is not that the searches are slow, it is that **their negative results are worthless**: with 14-53 frames of slack, h-first ordering has no signal, a beam drops the WR's own line (8-4 at layer ~40 regardless of root, F138; 1-2 at step 25), and every dry is uninformative (runbook §7.2). **The fix is one mechanism, not three: a wall-aware, y-coupled x bound** — `max x gain in k steps` must account for solid faces at Mario's body height that force a speed reset, and it must be coupled to y because he can jump over them. That subsumes `P2.2f-bound`'s end-game table and applies unchanged to both of 1-2's clusters. **Admissibility is non-negotiable and testable**: `tools/ygate_audit.py` / `tools/heuristic_audit.py` are the harnesses (existing records: 1,906,600 x-bound checks and 1,485 ygate checks, 0 violations). | V (h profiles: `runs/P2.2f/wr_hprofile.txt` 267 steps; 1-2's from `runs/P2.3e/joint_beam_d199c.log`) | `docs/experiments/P2.3e-framerule-scan.md` §34-36; `third_party/smb-opt/src/heuristics/{xpos.rs,ygate.rs}` |

| F202 | **The biggest RAM-write prize on this route ($06D6 WarpZoneControl in 1-2) sits 7 bytes above the only proven OOB writer's reach - so H7(c) reduces to one sharp question: is the $06CF bound tight?** F43(c): `WarpZoneControl` ($06D6) in {2,6} at a 1-2 pipe warps to 8-1, skipping 4-1 and 4-2 entirely - by far the largest saving available from a single byte. H5 refuted reaching those values through the game's own transitions (WZC in {0,1,4,5,6} at any pipe), so it requires a **write**. P3.1 found exactly one genuine OOB write mechanism in the game - block-buffer writes for objects above the status bar - with a bounded reach of **$05E0-$06CF**. $06D6 is **7 bytes past that ceiling**. | F203 | **P3.1's $06CF OOB reach bound is TIGHT — the block-buffer write mechanism cannot reach $06D6 (WarpZoneControl), and misses it by exactly 7 bytes.** Code-level proof over `data/disasm/smbdis.asm`. The mechanism is `sta ($06),y` where `$06/$07` are set by `GetBlockBufferAddr` (4328). Three bounds, each verified at every site: **(1) `$07` is always $05.** It is loaded from `BlockBufferAddr+2,y` — a **2-entry** table indexed by `tay` on a **nybble** (`lsr` x4), which looks like a live OOB table read but is not: `y = A >> 4` is bounded to {0,1} at both call sites. Call site 2 (`BlockBufferCollision`, 13069) constructs `A = (page_lsb << 4) | (x >> 4) <= 31` by `ora`/`ror`/`lsr` x3. Call site 1 (`RendBBuf`, 3266) passes `BlockBufferColumnPos` ($06A0), whose only two writers keep it <= $1F: the area initializer (2680) does `and #%00000001` **before** its four `asl`s, giving 0 or $10 (A there is `GetScreenPosition`'s return, but it is masked to bit 0 regardless), and `IncrementColumnPos` (3069) does `and #%00011111` after every `inc`. **(2) `$06` <= $DF.** `GetBlockBufferAddr` does `pla / and #%00001111 / adc BlockBufferAddr,y`, so the low byte is `{$00|$D0} + col` with col <= 15; $D0 + $0F = $DF, no carry into $07. **(3) `y` <= $F0 = 240.** The object path computes `y = ((SprObject_Y_Position + BlockBuffer_Y_Adder) & #%11110000) - $20` (13074-13078); `& $F0` yields $00..$F0, so after `sbc #$20` the wrapped values are $D0 (row 13), $E0 and **$F0 = 240 (max)**. The area-parser path cannot go OOB at all: its `y` starts at 0 and steps `adc #$10` under `cpx #$0d` (3283-3288), so `y <= 192` and its max address is $069F = exactly the end of Block_Buffer_2. **Therefore max effective address = $0500 + $DF + $F0 = $06CF**, an arithmetic maximum, not an estimate. **Consequence:** H7(c) — the biggest single-byte prize on the route (`$06D6` WarpZoneControl in {2,6} at a 1-2 pipe warps to 8-1, skipping 4-1 and 4-2, per F43/H5) — is **unreachable by the only OOB writer P3.1 found**. This closes one mechanism against one target; it does **not** refute H7, which stays open on P3.1 §4's own uncovered paths (stack overflow/underflow, non-indexed writes, `VRAM_Buffer` indexed by `VRAM_Buffer1_Offset`). It also rules the mechanism out for `$075F` WorldNumber, which sits 144 bytes above the ceiling. | V (code-level argument, disassembly labels + addresses cited) | `data/disasm/smbdis.asm` lines 2680-2687, 3266-3288, 4324-4341, 5462-5470, 13069-13079; refines `docs/oob-audit.md` §2/§4; `docs/experiments/P3.2-ram-oracle.md` §8 |

Only the last task terminates, and only conditionally: `PlayerEndWorld` (1231) does `ldy WorldNumber / cpy #World8 / bcs EndChkBButton` — at WorldNumber >= 7 the player is done with the game (the B-button world-select/`TerminateGame` path), and **below 7 it resets `AreaNumber`/`LevelNumber`, `inc WorldNumber`, and sets `OperMode` back to game mode for the next world.** **Consequence for F200's oracle predicate:** a real ending requires `OperMode == 2` **and** `WorldNumber >= 7`. On the WR route the distinction is vacuous — the route contains exactly one castle, 8-4, where WorldNumber is already 7 (F201) — which is why the session-17 band-A sweeps launched before this fix remain sound (an axe below world 8 cannot occur on the unperturbed route, and the unfixed binary can only *mis-label* a hit, never miss one, since every ending is preceded by `OperMode == 2`). Off-route it is not vacuous, so `ram_oracle` now requires WorldNumber >= 7 for `VICTORY` and records any sub-world-8 axe in a separate `axe_frame` column while continuing the run — an off-route axe being itself a notable event (F201/D2). | V (disassembly + re-run controls) | `data/disasm/smbdis.asm` 1112-1150, 1231-1246; `src/fastcore/ram_oracle.c`; controls re-pass after the change (positive `VICTORY` at 15056, null `CONVERGED(noop)` at 0) |

An overflow would therefore require either a call chain deeper than ~128 nested `jsr`s or a routine that `pha`s without a matching `pla` inside a loop; SMB1 is a flat state machine with no recursion (611 `jsr` sites, no self-referential call cycle known). **Scope: this is a code-level argument, not an exhaustive proof** — a proof-grade closure needs a static call-graph maximum-depth analysis and a pha/pla balance check per routine. | F221 | **The VRAM-overflow class (F218) is bounded: the amplifier is a +10 path with only three once-per-frame callers, one of them self-gated — reaching the block-object variables needs ~23 stacked writes and no path provides them.** Completing the F218 residual. **The amplifier found:** `MoveVOffset` (2055) does `adc #10 / jmp SetVRAMOffset` — a **10-byte** advance, far larger than the 6/7-byte screen-routine adds, and it is the tail of `WriteBlockMetatile`, so **every block-metatile write costs 10 bytes of VRAM buffer**. Summing every enumerated contributor as if all fired together lands in the 40-70 range — consistent with the observed max of **67** over 18,268 frames. Reaching `Block_BBuf_Low` at `$03e6` requires offset **227**, i.e. roughly **23 stacked 10-byte writes in a single frame**, and no enumerated path produces more than about three. **Scope:** caller-set enumeration plus arithmetic, cross-checked against the dump census — strong, and materially better than F218's bare empirical bound, but still not a machine-checked exhaustive proof. | F264 | **The area-load lag frame is `InitializeMemory`'s 1,868-byte RAM clear overrunning the NMI by ~19 %, and it is irreducible. L7/E6 carry no `--goal-ram` at all, so they take the `best_last` branch, not `best_frame`. **Conclusion: L4 was the outlier — the newest launcher, and the only one whose site has multiple pipes without qualification. | F277 | **L10: the other nine `PlayerFacingDir`/`Player_MovingDir` readers are audited, and the vine stays the only one that reads past its table into something that moves Mario.** Sites smbdis 5989, 6152, 6184, 6208, 6346, 6396, 9479, 12079, 14612, each checked against the table's real size in the listing. **In range by construction:** `ClimbAdderLow`/`ClimbAdderHigh` (5989) are **4 bytes** each (`$0e,$04,$fc,$f2` / `$00,$00,$ff,$ff`) for an index built 0-3, so facing 3 is already covered; `MaxLeftXSpdData` (3 B, y<=2), `MaxRightXSpdData` (**4** B, the 4th being the pipe-intro `$0c`, y<=3) and `FrictionData` (3 B) at 6184; `PlayerAnimTmrData` (3 B) at 6208. **Not indices at all:** 6152 and 6184 use facing/moving dir only in `cmp`; 6396 tests d0 with `lsr` (3 behaves as 1); 12079 tests with `dey/bne`; 14612 uses `and` for a graphics offset. **One genuine second out-of-table read:** 6346's `FireballXSpdData,y` with y = facing-1 = 2 against a **2-byte** table (`.db $40,$c0`) — real, but it lands only in `Fireball_X_Speed` and needs Fiery Mario, which this route never becomes. | V (ROM listing, all sites read) | `docs/experiments/H12-input-semantics.md` §8-10; `data/disasm/smbdis.asm` |
| F278 | **`Player_MovingDir` can hold 3, it cancels both known L+R speed penalties, and it defeats itself — a code-level negative, not a search one.** `ProcSkid` (smbdis 6217, `lda PlayerFacingDir / sta Player_MovingDir`) is the **only** writer in the game that can store a 3; the main writer `SetMoveDir` (5604) stores only `$01` or `$02`. Its conditions are reachable: hold L+R (so `SavedJoypadBits & $03` = 3, which never equals a normal moving direction, so the skid branch is taken) with `Player_XSpeedAbsolute < $0b`. **Two speed-relevant comparisons then flip to equal:** (a) `X_Physics` (6152) `lda Left_Right_Buttons / cmp Player_MovingDir / bne ChkRFast` — L+R normally fails this (3 vs 1/2) and drops to `ChkRFast`, losing the top cap; with moving dir 3 it **matches**, keeping the path that reaches `GetXPhy` at **Y=0** = `MaxRightXSpdData[0]` = the **40 cap**; (b) `GetXPhy` (6184) `lda PlayerFacingDir / cmp Player_MovingDir / beq ExitPhy` — facing 3 vs moving dir 3 **matches**, skipping the `asl FrictionAdderLow` that doubles friction under L+R. **Why it still cannot pay, from the same listing:** the writer continues `lda #$00 / sta Player_X_Speed / sta Player_X_MoveForce`, so the state cannot be entered with speed; and `SetMoveDir` runs every frame after `PlayerMovementSubs` with `ldy Player_X_Speed / beq PlayerSubs`, so the store is skipped **only** at exactly zero speed — the first frame with any horizontal speed overwrites moving dir back to 1/2. Net: **at most one frame of favourable branches, bought with the player's entire horizontal speed.** | V (code-level argument) | `docs/experiments/H12-input-semantics.md` §9; smbdis 5604, 6152, 6184, 6208-6221 |
| F279 | **CORRECTED (same session, before it was acted on). The `PlayerFacingDir == 1` gate at smbdis 12079 belongs to the SIDE-collision pipe metatiles, NOT to the vertical pipe entry that L4 is goaled on — and the vertical pipe's real L/R interaction is a different mechanism entirely, already correctly modelled.** (a) `CheckSideMTiles` -> `ChkPBtm` (12079) does `ldy PlayerFacingDir / dey / bne StopPlayerMove` and then tests for `$6c` (sideways-pipe bottom) and `$1f` (water-pipe bottom). That path gates on facing, but it is the *side* collision handler for those two metatiles. (b) **The vertical pipe entry — the one that sets `GameEngineSubroutine = 3`, which is L4's goal — is `HandlePipeEntry` (12270), reached from `LandPlyr` in the *bottom* collision path, and it contains NO facing check at all**: it requires `Up_Down_Buttons & $04` (Down held), `$00 == $11` (right foot on warp-pipe-right) and `$01 == $10` (left foot on warp-pipe-left). (c) **L/R does block it, but via the Down-nullification at smbdis 5584, not via facing:** `and #$04 / beq SizeChk / lda Player_State / bne SizeChk / ldy Left_Right_Buttons / beq SizeChk / lda #$00 / sta Left_Right_Buttons / sta Up_Down_Buttons` — pressing Down **while on the ground** with any left/right held zeroes **both** direction bytes, so `HandlePipeEntry`'s Down test fails. Airborne, the nullification is skipped (`lda Player_State / bne SizeChk`). (d) **The `smb-opt` model already encodes exactly this** and is correct: `emu.rs:523` guards the pipe entry with `(!self.started_on_ground || self.joypad_lr.is_empty()) && cv == 0x10 && right_foot_on_vert_pipe` — the same airborne-or-no-LR disjunction the ROM produces. **No model gap, and no correctness issue for L4** (which runs the real QuickNES core regardless). The practical rule for a vertical pipe stands, just for the right reason: **grounded, you must press Down with no left/right held.** | V (ROM listing + model source) | smbdis 5578-5589, 12079, 12270; `third_party/smb-opt/src/emu.rs:500-527`; `docs/experiments/H12-input-semantics.md` §10 |

| F281 | **Second instance in one session of the same defect class: two launchers for the same job drifted, and the divergence cost real compute silently.** `runs/L7-w84/launch.sh` (Linux) has supported `ONLY=` / `SKIP=` since it was written; `runs/L7-w84/launch_mac.sh` **never did**. When E9b exited and the queued waiter fired at 2026-08-26T05:08:20Z, the `SKIP='r3 r5'` it had been given was **accepted and silently ignored**, so it launched all five roots — including r3 and r5 at the same `--cells 80000` and **the same seeds (83, 85)** as the two already running on Linux. Same binary (byte-identical per F248), same parameters, same seed ⇒ byte-identical rollouts: **two of the Mac's five slots doing work that could not produce one new anomaly.** Caught within ~5 minutes of the launch by reading the launcher rather than the log — the logs looked perfectly healthy. Fixed: `ONLY`/`SKIP` added to the Mac launcher plus a **`SEEDADD`** offset that also renames the tag, so re-running an already-covered root is independent coverage instead of a duplicate; the two duplicates were killed (logs kept as `r{3,5}_dup_killed.log`) and relaunched as `r3s100` / `r5s100` at seeds 183/185. **This is the same class as the `CELLS` 60000-vs-80000 split found hours earlier** (the Mac launcher's default silently under-sizing r1–r4 by 25 %). Standing rule to add to PROCESS: **when a job moves machines, diff the two launchers before trusting a single number** — the failure mode is silent under-delivery, never an error. | V (launcher source + `queued_mac.log`) | `runs/L7-w84/launch_mac.sh`, `runs/L7-w84/queued_mac.log`, `runs/L7-w84/launch_s22_indep.log` |
